# EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_AUDIT_INTAKE

Revision `EBPM-R1-2026-09-06`; research cut-off `2026-09-06`.

## Purpose and authority boundary

This is an intake for a later, separate audit of an unspecified real system. It contains questions, not findings about that system. A candidate’s presence does not establish a gap, require a tool or authorise change. One existing mechanism may address several candidates; no rule requires one new control or owner per source property.

## Population and coverage

73 candidates; 73 examined; 53 `EXAMINED` and 20 `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`; 58 conditionally crosswalk-worthy, 14 non-retained and one unresolved. All ten mandatory families were examined. The corpus comprises 48 works with distinct source roles and 22 empirical/design-study summaries, not 48 independent effectiveness studies.

| Primary disposition | Candidates |
|---|---:|
| `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE` | 5 |
| `CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY` | 3 |
| `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT` | 17 |
| `DOMAIN_SPECIFIC` | 2 |
| `NO_GENERAL_PROPERTY` | 4 |
| `REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED` | 6 |
| `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM` | 22 |
| `STRONGLY_RETAINED` | 9 |
| `SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM` | 1 |
| `UNRESOLVED` | 1 |
| `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_BUREAUCRATISED` | 1 |
| `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED` | 2 |
| **Total** | **73** |

## Scope and evidence rules

**Scope.** Independent, plural and criticism-tested BPM study covering B1–B10 and within-tradition composition. The field is treated as organisational and socio-technical as well as analytical and executable.

**Boundaries.** No requester repositories, sibling Evolved corpora, runtime designs or target-specific adoption claims were consulted., Workflow and process mining are examined at documented BPM interfaces; complete algorithmic, formal and implementation histories of those fields are outside this lane., Industrial/quality antecedents are traced through explicit founding-source discussion, not a claimed complete independent history of every antecedent school., Business analysis, enterprise architecture and project management are distinguished at the problem/unit/decision boundary; their complete professional standards are not reconstructed., No jurisdiction-specific legal compliance or sector-specific clinical/safety procedure is prescribed., No effect-size meta-analysis, population prevalence estimate or complete database-indexed literature census is claimed. The denominator is the examined analytical candidate population., Live products are not benchmarked as current commercial recommendations. Standards versions are distinct from implementation adoption., Publication/deposit/issue/event dates are distinguished; the future 2026 BPM conference is not completed empirical evidence.

Use historical sources for ancestry, formal work for conditional model guarantees, standards for specified obligations, empirical studies for their measured settings, and research agendas for proposed questions. Do not infer adoption from a specification, benefit from adoption, causation from an association, or deployment safety from a modelling benchmark. Source-level units and validity threats are in [EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_SOURCE_TABLE.json](EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_SOURCE_TABLE.json).

Strong-theory/weak-deployment example: EBPM-073 offers encoded declarative consistency/history checks, not validation of the human constraints or complete event mapping. Field-supported but uncertain-general examples include process ownership, workaround inquiry, redesign heuristics and socio-technical enactment. Simulation, causal inference, event provenance and AI assistance require assumption-sensitive transfer. Maturity and ESG studies cannot substitute for a target’s observed outcomes. [S016; S018–S027; S033; S040; S042]

## Permitted audit outcomes

`ALREADY_ADDRESSED`, `INAPPLICABLE`, `SIMPLER_MECHANISM_SUFFICIENT`, `CONDITIONAL_IMPROVEMENT_OPPORTUNITY`, `EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT`, `UNRESOLVED_RESEARCH_QUESTION`, and `NO_UNCONDITIONAL_OBLIGATION` are all legitimate. Establish the trigger before judging the implementation. Preserve uncertainty rather than converting every unanswered question into a defect.

## Conditional candidate dossiers

The following 58 dossiers are usable independently of this conversation. Their domain profiles retain purpose, affected parties, work evidence, modelling purpose, measures, causal limits, authority, discretion, automation boundaries and revision triggers. Exact records and evidence partitions remain in the linked ledger.

### EBPM-001 — End-to-end purposes and beneficiaries

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-001`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/0`.

**Mature form.** Identify the intended result, its recipients and the end-to-end work that produces it. Make competing outcome claims visible before choosing measures or redesigning tasks; a purchaser’s preference is not automatically every affected person’s interest.

**Controlled failure.** A powerful buyer captures the purpose definition; broad aspirations are too vague to evaluate.

**Trigger.** A process is being selected, assessed or changed and local success may differ from the received outcome.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For a small, already understood service, a short agreed purpose and completion statement plus sample cases is sufficient; no portfolio exercise is required.

**Prerequisites.** An identifiable beneficiary or affected constituency; a feasible way to resolve competing objectives; evidence of actual results.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A process is being selected, assessed or changed and local success may differ from the received outcome.; consumer and authority: Responsible managers can commit resources; beneficiaries and affected workers contribute outcome evidence. Analysts cannot decide whose interests prevail merely by drawing the boundary.

**Authority boundary.** Responsible managers can commit resources; beneficiaries and affected workers contribute outcome evidence. Analysts cannot decide whose interests prevail merely by drawing the boundary.

**Required communication.** Recipients, performers and sponsors compare what they regard as successful completion and record unresolved differences.

**Observable postcondition.** A proposed change can be judged against an explicit outcome and identified recipients, rather than only a department’s activity count.

**Criticism.** S030 supplies a normative challenge to efficiency-only objectives; analytically, naming a customer does not justify excluding non-paying affected people.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The work from a meaningful initiating need to a received result, with explicit exclusions.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Customers or service recipients, frontline workers, upstream/downstream participants and indirectly affected people.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare the declared purpose with representative received results and complaints; a purpose statement alone is not evidence of fulfilment.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Purpose statement, case examples and an outcome/actor map; a flow diagram is optional.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Received outcome and burdens, not merely completed activities.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Identify the intended result, its recipients and the end-to-end work that produces it. Make competing outcome claims visible before choosing measures or redesigning tasks; a purchaser’s preference is not automatically every affected person’s interest.; causal limit: S030 supplies a normative challenge to efficiency-only objectives; analytically, naming a customer does not justify excluding non-paying affected people.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Responsible managers can commit resources; beneficiaries and affected workers contribute outcome evidence. Analysts cannot decide whose interests prevail merely by drawing the boundary.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Discretion may be necessary to achieve the purpose when a rule’s normal conditions fail.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automation must be judged against the same received outcome and affected parties.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Changed beneficiary needs, complaints, displaced harm or new strategic commitments.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Compare the declared purpose with representative received results and complaints; a purpose statement alone is not evidence of fulfilment. Measure: Received outcome and burdens, not merely completed activities.

**Anti-ceremony and retirement boundary.** protected function: A proposed change can be judged against an explicit outcome and identified recipients, rather than only a department’s activity count.; consumer: Responsible managers can commit resources; beneficiaries and affected workers contribute outcome evidence. Analysts cannot decide whose interests prevail merely by drawing the boundary.; minimal adequate alternative: For a small, already understood service, a short agreed purpose and completion statement plus sample cases is sufficient; no portfolio exercise is required.; fuller form trigger: A process is being selected, assessed or changed and local success may differ from the received outcome.; prerequisites: An identifiable beneficiary or affected constituency; a feasible way to resolve competing objectives; evidence of actual results.; simplification risk: A powerful buyer captures the purpose definition; broad aspirations are too vague to evaluate.; omission or retirement condition: Omit when the stated trigger is absent, an existing/cheaper mechanism supplies the required function, or its risk/consumer/dependency has disappeared. Reconsider on: Changed beneficiary needs, complaints, displaced harm or new strategic commitments.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

**Conflicts and coupling.** Tensions: T03 Relations: R001, R011, R039, R044, R052. Inspect their guards and incompatible assumptions in the composition model; no unlisted conflict is asserted absent merely because it was not registered.

#### Evidence partitions

**HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE: MODERATE_TO_STRONG_DOCUMENTARY.** The cited source documents the formulation or documented import; shared vocabulary alone is not treated as influence. Sources: S001, S002, S005

**FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL: MODERATE_CONCEPTUAL_OR_ANALYTICAL.** The mechanism is conceptually or analytically supported; no general formal theorem of organisational success is asserted. Sources: S001, S002, S005

**COMPARATIVE_EMPIRICAL: ABSENT_DIRECT_FOR_EXACT_PROPERTY.** No direct controlled comparison of this exact property is established by the inspected sources; conceptual retention is not a fabricated effect estimate. Sources: None; absence is qualified by the accompanying record.

**FIELD_OR_DEPLOYMENT: LIMITED_CONTEXTUAL.** Field/case evidence informs feasibility, failure or context; its sample and transfer threats remain in the source-table design records. Sources: S001, S002

**INDEPENDENT_REPLICATION: NOT_ESTABLISHED_FOR_EXACT_CLAIM.** Repeated papers, benchmark logs, related cases and development/evaluation stages are not added as independent replications. This is a limit of the inspected corpus, not proof that no replication exists anywhere. Sources: None; absence is qualified by the accompanying record.

**CONTRARY_FINDINGS: MATERIAL_AND_ADJUDICATED.** S030 supplies a normative challenge to efficiency-only objectives; analytically, naming a customer does not justify excluding non-paying affected people. Sources: S030

**TRANSFERABILITY: REQUIRES_NEW_CONTEXT_EVIDENCE.** Check the stated case structure, data, authority, users, protected outcomes and external effects. A model, survey association or single-case result does not automatically transfer to a new organisation. Sources: S001, S002, S005, S030

### EBPM-002 — Boundary accountability and displaced burdens

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-002`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/1`.

**Mature form.** Trace consequential work, waiting and risk beyond the unit receiving the improvement credit. Compare the whole affected path and require an explicit account of costs displaced onto partners, customers or unlogged workers.

**Controlled failure.** Boundary expansion becomes endless; an aggregate hides a harmed subgroup; partners conceal costs.

**Trigger.** A local measure improves, work is outsourced, or a redesigned interface changes another actor’s burden.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Inspect a few complete cases and ask receiving participants what changed. A full network model is unnecessary when interfaces and effects are simple.

**Prerequisites.** Access to receiving-side evidence or an explicit evidence limit; comparable case scope; identified boundary responsibilities.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A local measure improves, work is outsourced, or a redesigned interface changes another actor’s burden.; consumer and authority: A local owner may change its own work but cannot bind a partner unilaterally. Cross-boundary obligations require agreement with the actors bearing them.

**Authority boundary.** A local owner may change its own work but cannot bind a partner unilaterally. Cross-boundary obligations require agreement with the actors bearing them.

**Required communication.** Exchange completion meanings, demand, delays and burden evidence across interfaces; preserve disagreements rather than collapsing them into a local average.

**Observable postcondition.** An apparent local gain is accepted, qualified or rejected with its downstream and affected-person consequences visible.

**Criticism.** S028 validates a representation rather than network outcomes; S041 shows a contractually useful account can differ from work. The burden check is an analytical extension, not a proven universal protocol.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Local process plus materially affected upstream, downstream and external interfaces.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** The unit claiming benefit and the parties whose time, risk or unpaid work may increase.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Inspect receiving-side cases and off-system effort; do not infer absence from missing local logs.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Interface/burden map at the level needed for the intervention, not a complete enterprise model.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Total elapsed time, risk and workload by affected party and case class.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Trace consequential work, waiting and risk beyond the unit receiving the improvement credit. Compare the whole affected path and require an explicit account of costs displaced onto partners, customers or unlogged workers.; causal limit: S028 validates a representation rather than network outcomes; S041 shows a contractually useful account can differ from work. The burden check is an analytical extension, not a proven universal protocol.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** A local owner may change its own work but cannot bind a partner unilaterally. Cross-boundary obligations require agreement with the actors bearing them.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** A receiving actor may need to refuse or renegotiate an unsafe handoff.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** External effects of automated handoffs remain in scope even when the local task reports success.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** A partner backlog, complaint, unexplained abandonment or changed interface.

### EBPM-003 — Identifiable cases, triggers and completion

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-003`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/2`.

**Mature form.** State what initiates the work, what object or case is being advanced and what counts as completion, cancellation or continuing responsibility. Do not force a single stable case identifier or a terminal event onto open-ended knowledge work without justification.

**Controlled failure.** Artificial closure hides unresolved obligations; one event is attributed to the wrong case; open-ended work is distorted.

**Trigger.** Measurement, discovery or enactment depends on assigning work to cases and deciding whether work is finished.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use a lightweight episode or work-object account; where no stable repeated structure exists, coordinate goals and obligations without heavy process standardisation.

**Prerequisites.** A defensible unit of work; treatment of shared/multiple objects; an agreed completion meaning.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Measurement, discovery or enactment depends on assigning work to cases and deciding whether work is finished.; consumer and authority: Operational owners and recipients define business completion; a system administrator defines only the system’s representational boundary.

**Authority boundary.** Operational owners and recipients define business completion; a system administrator defines only the system’s representational boundary.

**Required communication.** Participants reconcile initiating events, shared objects and completion/cancellation meanings.

**Observable postcondition.** Cases used in measures or routing have a stated identity rule, with ambiguous or continuing cases identified.

**Criticism.** S014 challenges routine-case assumptions; S041 illustrates early work that a formal job identifier cannot represent.

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Cases, work objects or episodes and their creation/closure conditions.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare system case boundaries with work that begins before registration or continues after nominal closure.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Case definition or object relationship table; choose procedural/case-oriented detail only as warranted.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Completion, abandonment and outstanding obligations with explicit denominator rules.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** A workflow token ending is not necessarily real-world completion.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Unassignable events, shared objects, reopening or work outside the original start/end boundary.

### EBPM-004 — Selective process portfolio intervention

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-004`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/3`.

**Mature form.** Select interventions according to outcome importance, observed problems, dependencies and feasible change, not the convenience of what is already modelled. Include no intervention and deferred investigation as legitimate portfolio decisions.

**Controlled failure.** Scoring gives false precision; politically convenient projects displace important harms; prioritisation becomes a recurring ritual.

**Trigger.** Several process problems compete for limited analytical or change resources.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A direct, well-evidenced local repair can proceed without constructing an organisation-wide portfolio.

**Prerequisites.** A transparent prioritisation basis, bounded resource estimates and enough dependency knowledge to avoid contradictory initiatives.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Several process problems compete for limited analytical or change resources.; consumer and authority: Sponsors allocate scarce effort; affected process owners challenge feasibility and displacement assumptions.

**Authority boundary.** Sponsors allocate scarce effort; affected process owners challenge feasibility and displacement assumptions.

**Required communication.** Make selection reasons, dependencies and deferred concerns available to those whose work is prioritised or postponed.

**Observable postcondition.** Chosen and omitted interventions have intelligible reasons tied to expected consequences and capacity.

**Criticism.** S023 questions prescriptive maturity ladders. Analytically, portfolio completeness cannot justify neglecting an urgent concrete problem.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The set of candidate process interventions and their material dependencies.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Sponsors, process teams and beneficiaries of chosen and deferred work.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Selection uses observed problems as well as documented strategic claims.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A short prioritisation/dependency list; a process architecture is justified only when relationships require it.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Opportunity cost, feasible benefit and unresolved risk, not number of mapped processes.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Select interventions according to outcome importance, observed problems, dependencies and feasible change, not the convenience of what is already modelled. Include no intervention and deferred investigation as legitimate portfolio decisions.; causal limit: S023 questions prescriptive maturity ladders. Analytically, portfolio completeness cannot justify neglecting an urgent concrete problem.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Sponsors allocate scarce effort; affected process owners challenge feasibility and displacement assumptions.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Urgent exceptions may interrupt the portfolio order.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Tool acquisition is an option to compare, not an automatic portfolio objective.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** New material harm, changed dependencies or evidence invalidating priority assumptions.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Selection uses observed problems as well as documented strategic claims. Measure: Opportunity cost, feasible benefit and unresolved risk, not number of mapped processes.

### EBPM-005 — Process architecture with preserved dependencies

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-005`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/4`.

**Mature form.** Represent process families, decomposition and interfaces at the abstraction needed for coordination. Preserve cross-cutting dependencies when decomposing and adapt reference architectures to actual work rather than treating a borrowed map as an empirical description.

**Controlled failure.** Excessive decomposition hides cross-cutting work; a reference model is mistaken for actual practice.

**Trigger.** Several processes share resources, objects or interfaces and local changes can conflict.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For a small scope, an interface list with responsible participants can replace a full architecture hierarchy.

**Prerequisites.** Stable enough process distinctions; identified relationship types; a consumer able to use the architecture.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Several processes share resources, objects or interfaces and local changes can conflict.; consumer and authority: Enterprise/process coordinators maintain the map with local expertise; the architecture does not override functional or contractual authority.

**Authority boundary.** Enterprise/process coordinators maintain the map with local expertise; the architecture does not override functional or contractual authority.

**Required communication.** Local owners reconcile shared terms, boundaries and dependencies before treating components as independent.

**Observable postcondition.** An analyst can locate the affected process and its consequential relationships without equating reporting lines with work flow.

**Criticism.** S005’s context/simplicity principles challenge architecture for its own sake; S008 offers multiple approaches rather than a universal best map.

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A process portfolio and its decomposition, shared objects, resources and interfaces.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Validate architecture relationships against actual handoffs and shared work, not just approved documents.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Architecture, interface matrix or reference framework adapted to local evidence.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Dependency visibility and decision usefulness rather than diagram coverage.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Represent process families, decomposition and interfaces at the abstraction needed for coordination. **PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Enterprise/process coordinators maintain the map with local expertise; the architecture does not override functional or contractual authority.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Exceptions crossing decomposition boundaries must remain visible.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Application architecture is related but does not define the complete organisational process architecture.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** New shared resources, duplicated work, boundary disputes or changed interdependencies.

### EBPM-006 — Triangulated discovery of performed work

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-006`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/5`.

**Mature form.** Compare interviews, documents, walkthroughs, direct observation and event evidence where they reveal different aspects of work. Record provenance and disagreement; neither a remembered account, a prescription nor an event log is intrinsically the whole truth.

**Controlled failure.** Observation changes behaviour; workshop consensus conceals dissent; system logs omit essential coordination.

**Trigger.** A decision depends on what actually happens and available accounts may be incomplete, selective or incentive-distorted.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For a transparent low-risk process, a few representative walkthroughs with performers and recipients may suffice; all evidence channels are not compulsory.

**Prerequisites.** Access to relevant participants and cases; a sampling rationale; permission to observe work without disproportionate intrusion.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A decision depends on what actually happens and available accounts may be incomplete, selective or incentive-distorted.; consumer and authority: Analysts synthesise evidence; performers and recipients can correct it. Sponsorship does not entitle an analyst to dismiss inconvenient observations.

**Authority boundary.** Analysts synthesise evidence; performers and recipients can correct it. Sponsorship does not entitle an analyst to dismiss inconvenient observations.

**Required communication.** Bring discrepant accounts back to the people involved and distinguish uncertainty from disagreement about the desired process.

**Observable postcondition.** The as-is account identifies its sources, covered variants and consequential uncertainties.

**Criticism.** S041’s account of work outside workflow categories and S035’s data-quality principles defeat unqualified ground-truth claims. Triangulation can still share the same bias.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Observe exceptions without presuming that every deviation is either misconduct or innovation.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated records are one observation channel with instrumentation boundaries.

### EBPM-007 — Participant validation and explicit disagreement

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-007`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/6`.

**Mature form.** Test a representation with people who perform, receive and coordinate the work using concrete cases and exceptions. Preserve disagreement about facts, goals and authority separately; endorsement of a picture is not evidence of shared decision meaning.

**Controlled failure.** Power imbalances manufacture agreement; frontline time is consumed without influence; validation becomes sign-off theatre.

**Trigger.** A process model or redesign will be used across roles with different knowledge or incentives.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A brief case walkthrough or annotated narrative may establish the needed understanding without a workshop series.

**Prerequisites.** Relevant voices can participate safely; the model’s purpose is explicit; disagreements have a decision route.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A process model or redesign will be used across roles with different knowledge or incentives.; consumer and authority: Participants validate their knowledge, not necessarily the whole process; sponsors resolve authorised choices without erasing dissent.

**Authority boundary.** Participants validate their knowledge, not necessarily the whole process; sponsors resolve authorised choices without erasing dissent.

**Required communication.** Ask participants to explain what happens in discriminating cases and identify omissions or conflicting interpretations.

**Observable postcondition.** Users can apply the representation to the relevant case distinction, or a specific unresolved disagreement is recorded.

**Criticism.** S030 raises affected-person and fairness issues; S041 shows one representation can serve different organisational purposes. Consensus is not a universal correctness test.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Case-based validation checks stated models against participants’ lived work and observed evidence.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A model, narrative or table whose decisions and exceptions users can explain.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Correct application to consequential examples and visible unresolved disagreements.

### EBPM-008 — Variant and exception coverage in discovery

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-008`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/7`.

**Mature form.** Sample materially different case types, failure paths and informal coordination. State which variants are covered and which remain uncertain; do not infer that rare or unlogged work is irrelevant simply because it is absent from a convenient sample.

**Controlled failure.** Rare catastrophic cases disappear in averages; sampling only completed cases excludes abandonment.

**Trigger.** Case mix, exceptions or hidden handoffs could change analysis or redesign conclusions.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use a few deliberately contrasting cases when the variant space is small; exhaustive enumeration is unnecessary.

**Prerequisites.** A risk-relevant variant distinction and access to at least some atypical cases or informed accounts.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Case mix, exceptions or hidden handoffs could change analysis or redesign conclusions.; consumer and authority: Process investigators select cases with performers and recipients; owners accept the resulting uncertainty or commission further inquiry.

**Authority boundary.** Process investigators select cases with performers and recipients; owners accept the resulting uncertainty or commission further inquiry.

**Required communication.** Communicate normal-path coverage separately from exception and missing-case coverage.

**Observable postcondition.** A redesign claim states the case classes it covers and the exceptions that could defeat it.

**Criticism.** S014 and S041 demonstrate that routine models can omit consequential variation. Analytically, exhaustive variant modelling can cost more than responsive case handling.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Case classes, exceptions, abandoned work and informal coordination within the intervention scope.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** People whose cases differ from the dominant workflow and workers handling unusual demand.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Separate observed frequency from plausible-but-unobserved exceptions and participant recollections.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Variant table, exception examples or a richer process/case model when needed.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Coverage and harm by case class rather than only an aggregate mean.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Sample materially different case types, failure paths and informal coordination. State which variants are covered and which remain uncertain; do not infer that rare or unlogged work is irrelevant simply because it is absent from a convenient sample.; causal limit: S014 and S041 demonstrate that routine models can omit consequential variation. Analytically, exhaustive variant modelling can cost more than responsive case handling.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Process investigators select cases with performers and recipients; owners accept the resulting uncertainty or commission further inquiry.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Legitimate exceptional action is investigated on its purpose and constraints.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automation may cover only a declared subset of variants, with a usable manual route.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** New case mix, abandonment, recurring exception or an adverse outlier.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Separate observed frequency from plausible-but-unobserved exceptions and participant recollections. Measure: Coverage and harm by case class rather than only an aggregate mean.

### EBPM-009 — Event-data provenance and observability limits

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-009`. Disposition: `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/8`.

**Mature form.** Establish event meaning, case/object attribution, timestamp semantics, extraction scope and known omissions before using event evidence. Keep data-processing transformations traceable and expose activities that the instrumentation cannot observe.

**Controlled failure.** Registration time is confused with work time; multi-object events are duplicated; workarounds become falsely labelled fraud.

**Trigger.** Event data will justify an as-is claim, comparison, forecast or intervention.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A small manually checked sample and extraction description may be sufficient; a mining platform is not required.

**Prerequisites.** Understandable source-system events, lawful/appropriate data access, and enough manual or independent evidence to test interpretation.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Event data will justify an as-is claim, comparison, forecast or intervention.; consumer and authority: Data custodians explain instrumentation; analysts own inference limits; process owners decide whether evidence is adequate for action.

**Authority boundary.** Data custodians explain instrumentation; analysts own inference limits; process owners decide whether evidence is adequate for action.

**Required communication.** Exchange data definitions, missingness and case-attribution assumptions with performers and consumers of the analysis.

**Observable postcondition.** Claims derived from logs specify what was recorded, transformed and excluded, including consequential blind spots.

**Criticism.** S018 explicitly assumes complete consistent event data, while S025 and S041 expose work beyond system records. More rows do not repair invalid semantics.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The mapping from real work/events to the analytical case and activity schema.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Data subjects, performers, custodians and people affected by log-based judgements.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Logged work is distinguished from work performed; extraction and selection are part of the evidence claim.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Event dictionary and provenance/sample checks linked to any discovered model.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Data completeness/consistency and outcome relevance, not raw log volume.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Establish event meaning, case/object attribution, timestamp semantics, extraction scope and known omissions before using event evidence. Keep data-processing transformations traceable and expose activities that the instrumentation cannot observe.; causal limit: S018 explicitly assumes complete consistent event data, while S025 and S041 expose work beyond system records. More rows do not repair invalid semantics.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Data custodians explain instrumentation; analysts own inference limits; process owners decide whether evidence is adequate for action.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Exceptional action may occur off-system and needs careful interpretation.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Instrumentation and access controls define observation boundaries, not the whole process.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Schema changes, inconsistent timestamps, missing cases or discrepancies with human accounts.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Logged work is distinguished from work performed; extraction and selection are part of the evidence claim. Measure: Data completeness/consistency and outcome relevance, not raw log volume.

### EBPM-010 — Purpose-fit representation and abstraction

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-010`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/9`.

**Mature form.** Choose the representation and abstraction by the decision it must support and the people who must use it. Preserve the distinctions needed for that task; a narrative, table, decision model, process graph or case representation can be adequate without being interchangeable.

**Controlled failure.** A communication sketch is reused as executable truth; a technically exact model is unintelligible to its decision maker.

**Trigger.** A representation is being selected, simplified, shared or reused for a new purpose.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use the lightest already-understood representation that preserves the needed distinction; no diagram is required where a short narrative is enough.

**Prerequisites.** An identified consumer and task; a way to test comprehension or analytic/execution adequacy.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A representation is being selected, simplified, shared or reused for a new purpose.; consumer and authority: The intended users help judge fitness; a notation specialist cannot infer business authority from technical sophistication.

**Authority boundary.** The intended users help judge fitness; a notation specialist cannot infer business authority from technical sophistication.

**Required communication.** Explain what the representation omits and which uses it does not support.

**Observable postcondition.** The consumer can make the intended distinction, and prohibited or unsupported uses are visible.

**Criticism.** S010 offers no significant benefit from guideline availability in its small experiment; S041 shows different useful representational roles. Appearance is not fitness.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A model’s intended use rather than an assumed universal representation of all work.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Model authors, operational users, learners, implementers and external accountability consumers.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** The model’s relation to actual, prescribed and hypothetical work must be named.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Purpose-fit narrative, table, BPMN/EPC, decision, case or constraint representation.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Task-relevant understanding or analysis correctness, not visual elegance alone.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Choose the representation and abstraction by the decision it must support and the people who must use it. Preserve the distinctions needed for that task; a narrative, table, decision model, process graph or case representation can be adequate without being interchangeable.; causal limit: S010 offers no significant benefit from guideline availability in its small experiment; S041 shows different useful representational roles. Appearance is not fitness.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** The intended users help judge fitness; a notation specialist cannot infer business authority from technical sophistication.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Represent decision/discretion boundaries when users need them, not only normal sequence.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Execution requires a distinct implementation contract beyond a communicative representation.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** A new consumer, new task, misunderstanding or omitted consequential distinction.

### EBPM-011 — Separate syntactic, semantic and pragmatic quality

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-011`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/10`.

**Mature form.** Check notation well-formedness separately from fidelity to the intended domain and understandability for the intended user. Use the corresponding test for each claim; one kind of validity cannot stand in for the others.

**Controlled failure.** Syntax success is sold as business truth; participant familiarity hides incorrect semantics.

**Trigger.** A model will justify a decision, communicate consequential instructions or support execution.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A short review against examples may suffice for a simple narrative; formal syntax checks apply only to notations that have such rules.

**Prerequisites.** Defined model purpose, domain evidence and representative user tasks; notation rules where applicable.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A model will justify a decision, communicate consequential instructions or support execution.; consumer and authority: Tool validators assess encoded rules; domain experts assess meaning; users demonstrate comprehension. None alone certifies the other dimensions.

**Authority boundary.** Tool validators assess encoded rules; domain experts assess meaning; users demonstrate comprehension. None alone certifies the other dimensions.

**Required communication.** Report quality findings by dimension and resolve disagreements with the relevant expertise.

**Observable postcondition.** A model’s validation statement says which quality dimensions were tested and what remains unestablished.

**Criticism.** S007 contains authoritative-model corrections and S010 a bounded null result. Analytically, a quality checklist can itself become a proxy for actual use.

### EBPM-012 — Behaviour-preserving complexity management

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-012`. Disposition: `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_BUREAUCRATISED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/11`.

**Mature form.** Reduce unnecessary representational complexity while preserving decision-relevant behaviour and relationships. Test the simplified form with its users; use modularisation or multiple coordinated views when a single small diagram would conceal essential interactions.

**Controlled failure.** Decomposition hides cross-boundary dependencies; adherence to a size rule increases navigation cost.

**Trigger.** Users cannot reliably interpret a model, or model changes introduce structural errors.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Remove an ambiguity or improve a label before imposing a new modelling standard or decomposition programme.

**Prerequisites.** A known task and user population; explicit behaviour or meaning to preserve; a comparison with the current representation.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Users cannot reliably interpret a model, or model changes introduce structural errors.; consumer and authority: Modellers propose changes; users and domain experts check that useful meaning has not been lost.

**Authority boundary.** Modellers propose changes; users and domain experts check that useful meaning has not been lost.

**Required communication.** Communicate the relation between simplified and detailed views and the exceptions intentionally retained.

**Observable postcondition.** Users handle the relevant cases at least adequately without loss of critical constraints or relationships.

**Criticism.** S010’s small guideline experiment did not establish the tested benefit; S044’s positive notation redesign has one-platform/cohort limits. Simpler-looking is not universally better.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The representation’s complexity relative to a specific user task.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Model consumers and maintainers, including non-specialists.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Simplification is checked against the underlying work/requirements rather than treated as discovery.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Structured diagram, modular views or plain description selected by observed comprehension.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Interpretation errors, task time and maintenance burden with preserved meaning.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Reduce unnecessary representational complexity while preserving decision-relevant behaviour and relationships. Test the simplified form with its users; use modularisation or multiple coordinated views when a single small diagram would conceal essential interactions.; causal limit: S010’s small guideline experiment did not establish the tested benefit; S044’s positive notation redesign has one-platform/cohort limits. Simpler-looking is not universally better.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Modellers propose changes; users and domain experts check that useful meaning has not been lost.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Do not simplify away meaningful exceptions or decision authority.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** An equivalent communicative simplification is not necessarily executable-equivalent.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Observed comprehension failures, growing model maintenance cost or changed user tasks.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Simplification is checked against the underlying work/requirements rather than treated as discovery. Measure: Interpretation errors, task time and maintenance burden with preserved meaning.

### EBPM-013 — Interpretable activity labels and domain terms

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-013`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/12`.

**Mature form.** Use terms that distinguish what action or decision occurs and what object is affected, in the intended users’ language. Resolve ambiguous domain terms before standardising a label grammar; test important labels through concrete cases.

**Controlled failure.** Mechanical verb–object rewriting obscures accepted specialist terms; tidy labels conceal undefined responsibilities.

**Trigger.** Users disagree about an activity, decision or completion label, or labels are reused across roles.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A brief glossary or example can be enough; a mandatory naming convention is unnecessary when existing terms work.

**Prerequisites.** Knowledge of the user vocabulary and the distinctions the model must communicate.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Users disagree about an activity, decision or completion label, or labels are reused across roles.; consumer and authority: Domain participants determine meaning; model maintainers ensure consistent use without overriding legitimate local terminology.

**Authority boundary.** Domain participants determine meaning; model maintainers ensure consistent use without overriding legitimate local terminology.

**Required communication.** Ask users what a label authorises or predicts and reconcile materially different readings.

**Observable postcondition.** Critical labels support the intended action/object distinction across the relevant users.

**Criticism.** S009’s recommendations and S044’s bounded usability evidence do not prove one grammatical pattern best for all cultures, languages or tasks.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The meaning of actions, decisions and objects in a representation.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** People who interpret or execute labelled work, including external partners.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Names are checked against actual activities and decision meanings, not only documents.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Labels with examples or a lightweight glossary; no universal naming grammar.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Consequential interpretation errors rather than convention-compliance percentage.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Use terms that distinguish what action or decision occurs and what object is affected, in the intended users’ language. Resolve ambiguous domain terms before standardising a label grammar; test important labels through concrete cases.; causal limit: S009’s recommendations and S044’s bounded usability evidence do not prove one grammatical pattern best for all cultures, languages or tasks.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Domain participants determine meaning; model maintainers ensure consistent use without overriding legitimate local terminology.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Labels must not imply that an optional judgement is a mandatory automatic action.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** A human-readable label does not specify executable service semantics.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Ambiguous use, a new audience or a term whose meaning changes with the process.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Names are checked against actual activities and decision meanings, not only documents. Measure: Consequential interpretation errors rather than convention-compliance percentage.

Reconsider on: Ambiguous use, a new audience or a term whose meaning changes with the process.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-014 — Explicit modelling-versus-execution contract

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-014`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/13`.

**Mature form.** Declare whether a model communicates, analyses or drives execution, and identify the additional implementation semantics and organisational arrangements needed to move between those uses. Keep engine events and real completion separate until their correspondence is demonstrated.

**Controlled failure.** Ignored non-operational elements conceal human obligations; tool import succeeds while semantics differ.

**Trigger.** A model is handed to implementers, imported into a tool or presented as evidence that a process operates correctly.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For a descriptive model, simply state that it is not executable; do not build an execution contract unnecessarily.

**Prerequisites.** Known notation/version and implementation subset where execution is intended; identified human/system interfaces.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A model is handed to implementers, imported into a tool or presented as evidence that a process operates correctly.; consumer and authority: Implementers control deployed semantics within their authority; process owners and recipients establish real obligations and completion criteria.

**Authority boundary.** Implementers control deployed semantics within their authority; process owners and recipients establish real obligations and completion criteria.

**Required communication.** Modellers, implementers and operators reconcile what a task or event means in each representation.

**Observable postcondition.** The model’s claimed scope matches tested implementation behaviour and does not silently certify organisational consequences.

**Criticism.** S011 explicitly distinguishes modelling and execution scopes. S027 and S041 show deployment and situated work are additional evidential problems.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Normative conformance is version-specific; external effects are not certified by model validation.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Engine migration, changed task semantics, ignored model elements or completion mismatch.

### EBPM-015 — Separable decision logic and decision authority

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-015`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/14`.

**Mature form.** Where decision rules vary independently of routing, distinguish the information/rules used to decide from the actor authorised to decide and from the consequences of that decision. Keep a traceable connection between a decision model and the process consuming its result.

**Controlled failure.** A well-formed rule table embeds an unjustified policy; a model prediction is mistaken for authorisation.

**Trigger.** Business decisions are obscured in flow diagrams, reused across processes or changed independently of task order.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A decision table or explicit rationale can be sufficient; DMN is an option rather than an obligation.

**Prerequisites.** Identifiable decision inputs, policy meaning, accountable authority and consumers of the decision.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Business decisions are obscured in flow diagrams, reused across processes or changed independently of task order.; consumer and authority: A rule author or prediction service is not automatically the legitimate decision maker; accountable actors retain domain-specific authority.

**Authority boundary.** A rule author or prediction service is not automatically the legitimate decision maker; accountable actors retain domain-specific authority.

**Required communication.** Communicate the rule/version, available inputs, discretion and meaning of the result to the consuming process.

**Observable postcondition.** A decision can be explained and changed without silently changing who may make it or what outcome follows.

**Criticism.** S013 was inspected only for specification identity. Detailed DMN semantics are not inferred; S030 supports the broader accountability concern, not a validated universal decision protocol.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A decision’s inputs, policy, result, authorised actor and consuming process.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Decision subjects, policy owners, decision makers and downstream implementers.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare declared rules with actual decisions, exceptions and received consequences.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Decision table/model linked to flow; DMN version semantics require separate verification if used.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Decision correctness under stated criteria, consistency and affected-person outcomes.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Where decision rules vary independently of routing, distinguish the information/rules used to decide from the actor authorised to decide and from the consequences of that decision. Keep a traceable connection between a decision model and the process consuming its result.; causal limit: S013 was inspected only for specification identity. Detailed DMN semantics are not inferred; S030 supports the broader accountability concern, not a validated universal decision protocol.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** A rule author or prediction service is not automatically the legitimate decision maker; accountable actors retain domain-specific authority.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Discretion and override conditions must not be erased by extracting a rule model.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated decision support is distinct from permission to impose its result.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Policy change, unexplained decisions, missing input or mismatch between modelled and actual authority.

### EBPM-016 — Knowledge-work and declarative alternatives

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-016`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/15`.

**Mature form.** Select support according to what can be known in advance: goals and information needs, mandatory constraints, planned tasks or a stable flow. Allow structured sub-processes inside knowledge work and avoid making flexibility equivalent to the absence of guidance.

**Controlled failure.** Discretion becomes unaccountable; a constraint model is too difficult to understand; supposedly flexible tools impose hidden rigidity.

**Trigger.** Cases require changing knowledge, professional judgement or runtime planning that an exhaustive flow would distort.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A shared case note, goal list and essential constraints can be enough without a case-management engine.

**Prerequisites.** Competent participants; intelligible goals/constraints; usable shared information and a way to recognise unresolved obligations.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Cases require changing knowledge, professional judgement or runtime planning that an exhaustive flow would distort.; consumer and authority: Case workers plan within professional and organisational authority; system flexibility does not grant new powers.

**Authority boundary.** Case workers plan within professional and organisational authority; system flexibility does not grant new powers.

**Required communication.** Exchange case knowledge, decisions and changes in responsibility rather than only next-task messages.

**Observable postcondition.** Participants can adapt the work while preserving essential obligations and keeping others informed.

**Criticism.** S014 is conceptual/illustrative and S012 normative. S042’s formal admissibility does not prove suitability for every knowledge worker. The formal checking mechanism is separately registered as EBPM-073.

### EBPM-017 — Outcome-valid, plural performance measures

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-017`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/16`.

**Mature form.** Use measures that represent the intended received outcome and material costs, with case mix and affected groups visible. Keep time, effort, quality, flexibility, resilience and fairness distinct where they can move in opposing directions; select justified constraints and priorities rather than pretending all are maximised.

**Controlled failure.** Proxy gaming, misleading averages, unmeasured harm and excessive measurement burden.

**Trigger.** Measures will guide resource allocation, redesign acceptance or individual/process appraisal.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A small set of outcome and harm checks can be sufficient; a comprehensive balanced dashboard is not mandatory.

**Prerequisites.** Defined purposes and boundaries, credible measures and an explicit rule for consequential trade-offs.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Measures will guide resource allocation, redesign acceptance or individual/process appraisal.; consumer and authority: Accountable decision makers choose priorities with affected-party input; analysts expose measurement limitations rather than choosing values by convenience.

**Authority boundary.** Accountable decision makers choose priorities with affected-party input; analysts expose measurement limitations rather than choosing values by convenience.

**Required communication.** Communicate the unit, denominator, case mix, uncertainty and parties bearing benefits/costs.

**Observable postcondition.** An improvement claim states which outcomes changed, which worsened and whose experience is represented.

**Criticism.** S031 and S040 measure reported constructs/practices rather than direct environmental gains; S026 and S030 challenge performance control and efficiency-only values.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** End-to-end outcomes and the parties affected by measured and unmeasured work.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Recipients, workers, partners, sponsors and people exposed to adverse consequences.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Check reported performance against actual experiences and off-system work.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A small measure definition set with case examples and explicit trade-offs.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Received outcomes, time/cost/quality/flexibility and relevant distributional or resilience effects.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Use measures that represent the intended received outcome and material costs, with case mix and affected groups visible. **HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Useful exception handling can worsen a conformance metric while improving the actual outcome.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Higher automated throughput does not validate the underlying decision or external result.

### EBPM-018 — Elapsed time distinguished from work effort

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-018`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/17`.

**Mature form.** Separate elapsed time experienced by the case or recipient from active work effort and resource occupancy. Locate waiting, transfer and rework contributions before attributing delay to an actor or deciding which activity to accelerate.

**Controlled failure.** Timestamps measure data entry, not work; parallel activity is double-counted; monitoring imposes intrusive effort tracking.

**Trigger.** A time-based performance claim or delay intervention is being made.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A manually reconstructed case timeline can be adequate; detailed time logging is unnecessary when the causal bottleneck is already clear.

**Prerequisites.** Consistent start/end and clock semantics; relevant case classes; treatment of overlapping work and unlogged waiting.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A time-based performance claim or delay intervention is being made.; consumer and authority: Analysts interpret time evidence with performers; owners can change allocation or handoff arrangements within their authority.

**Authority boundary.** Analysts interpret time evidence with performers; owners can change allocation or handoff arrangements within their authority.

**Required communication.** Show the timeline and distinguish work, queueing, handoff and interruption interpretations.

**Observable postcondition.** A delay claim no longer conflates a worker’s effort with the case’s elapsed duration.

**Criticism.** S017 cautions against simplifying human-resource behaviour. The distinction is sound, but accurate component attribution remains an empirical question.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A case’s initiating-to-completion duration and the work/resources contributing to it.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Recipients waiting for completion and performers whose labour may be misattributed.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare event timestamps with what the worker and recipient regard as actual activity and waiting.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Case timeline with overlap, waiting and uncertainty markers.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Elapsed duration, active effort, occupancy and rework separately.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Separate elapsed time experienced by the case or recipient from active work effort and resource occupancy. Locate waiting, transfer and rework contributions before attributing delay to an actor or deciding which activity to accelerate.; causal limit: S017 cautions against simplifying human-resource behaviour. The distinction is sound, but accurate component attribution remains an empirical question.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Analysts interpret time evidence with performers; owners can change allocation or handoff arrangements within their authority.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Legitimate interruption or consultation should not automatically count as poor worker performance.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated task speed may have little effect on external queues or recipient delay.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Timestamp changes, new concurrency, unexplained delay or a shift from work to waiting.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Compare event timestamps with what the worker and recipient regard as actual activity and waiting. Measure: Elapsed duration, active effort, occupancy and rework separately.

### EBPM-019 — Capacity, variability and bottleneck diagnosis

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-019`. Disposition: `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/18`.

**Mature form.** Examine demand, service variation, shared capacity and allocation rules together before adding resources or seeking maximum utilisation. Use a queueing model or simulation only when its assumptions represent the relevant work; recheck the end-to-end constraint after a local change.

**Controlled failure.** Average-rate assumptions hide bursts; human multitasking violates resource models; a bottleneck merely moves downstream.

**Trigger.** Persistent queues, fluctuating demand or shared-resource contention dominate outcomes.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A workload/timeline comparison and a bounded allocation trial may dominate building a detailed stochastic model.

**Prerequisites.** Meaningful arrival/service observations, resource availability and allocation assumptions; an appropriate steady-state or transient scope.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Persistent queues, fluctuating demand or shared-resource contention dominate outcomes.; consumer and authority: Resource managers can change capacity and assignment; analysts supply conditional predictions, not authority to alter staffing or worker obligations.

**Authority boundary.** Resource managers can change capacity and assignment; analysts supply conditional predictions, not authority to alter staffing or worker obligations.

**Required communication.** Make demand, capacity and behavioural assumptions explicit to those who allocate resources and bear workload.

**Observable postcondition.** The selected intervention targets a supported constraint and includes evidence about the resulting queue and displaced load.

**Criticism.** S017 and S018 show assumption dependence; S027 demonstrates data and organisational complications. A formal queue result is not automatically a valid model of people.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Queues, shared resources and capacity across the affected end-to-end path.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Waiting recipients, resource managers, workers and downstream teams.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Observed allocation and interruptions may differ from nominal staffing or scheduled capacity.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A simple workload model, queueing abstraction or simulation matched to the problem.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Waiting distribution, throughput, resource load and resilience under demand variation.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Examine demand, service variation, shared capacity and allocation rules together before adding resources or seeking maximum utilisation. Use a queueing model or simulation only when its assumptions represent the relevant work; recheck the end-to-end constraint after a local change.; causal limit: S017 and S018 show assumption dependence; S027 demonstrates data and organisational complications. A formal queue result is not automatically a valid model of people.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Resource managers can change capacity and assignment; analysts supply conditional predictions, not authority to alter staffing or worker obligations.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Human prioritisation and exceptional cases can change service order and must not be erased.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Faster automated arrivals may overload a human or partner bottleneck.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Demand drift, moved bottleneck, overload, altered prioritisation or degraded recovery capacity.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Observed allocation and interruptions may differ from nominal staffing or scheduled capacity. Measure: Waiting distribution, throughput, resource load and resilience under demand variation.

**Anti-ceremony and retirement boundary.** protected function: The selected intervention targets a supported constraint and includes evidence about the resulting queue and displaced load.; consumer: Resource managers can change capacity and assignment; analysts supply conditional predictions, not authority to alter staffing or worker obligations.; minimal adequate alternative: A workload/timeline comparison and a bounded allocation trial may dominate building a detailed stochastic model.; fuller form trigger: Persistent queues, fluctuating demand or shared-resource contention dominate outcomes.; prerequisites: Meaningful arrival/service observations, resource availability and allocation assumptions; an appropriate steady-state or transient scope.; simplification risk: Average-rate assumptions hide bursts; human multitasking violates resource models; a bottleneck merely moves downstream.; omission or retirement condition: Omit when the stated trigger is absent, an existing/cheaper mechanism supplies the required function, or its risk/consumer/dependency has disappeared. Reconsider on: Demand drift, moved bottleneck, overload, altered prioritisation or degraded recovery capacity.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-020 — Handoff and rework mechanism diagnosis

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-020`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/19`.

**Mature form.** Trace how a handoff, missing information or rejected output generates delay and repeated work. Distinguish avoidable rework from necessary checking, learning or repair before changing task boundaries or removing feedback.

**Controlled failure.** A feedback loop is labelled waste; work is shifted to the recipient; handoff elimination overloads one actor.

**Trigger.** Cases bounce between actors, repeatedly request information or suffer unexplained rejection/delay.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Walk a few failed cases and repair a single information or responsibility defect before redesigning the entire process.

**Prerequisites.** Case-level links between handoff, information and resulting rework; clarity about the function of any repeated step.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Cases bounce between actors, repeatedly request information or suffer unexplained rejection/delay.; consumer and authority: Sending and receiving actors jointly identify the mechanism; an owner needs authority or agreement to change both sides.

**Authority boundary.** Sending and receiving actors jointly identify the mechanism; an owner needs authority or agreement to change both sides.

**Required communication.** Exchange concrete rejected outputs, acceptance conditions and reasons for return, rather than blaming the next department.

**Observable postcondition.** A claimed rework reduction is tied to a changed causal mechanism and retained necessary controls.

**Criticism.** S016’s practitioner objections preserve training and segregation constraints; S025/41 show informal repairs may be essential rather than waste.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The handoff and feedback relationships that produce repeated work in a case.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Senders, receivers, customers and workers absorbing correction effort.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Trace actual returns and informal fixes rather than counting arrows in a diagram.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A handoff/acceptance map with failed-case examples.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Repeated effort, delay, error escape and burden transferred between participants.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Trace how a handoff, missing information or rejected output generates delay and repeated work. Distinguish avoidable rework from necessary checking, learning or repair before changing task boundaries or removing feedback.; causal limit: S016’s practitioner objections preserve training and segregation constraints; S025/41 show informal repairs may be essential rather than waste.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Sending and receiving actors jointly identify the mechanism; an owner needs authority or agreement to change both sides.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Manual repair may be legitimate while revealing a recurring design problem.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automation can accelerate the creation and circulation of defective work.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Repeated rejection, new error propagation or apparent rework reduction with rising external complaints.
