### EBPM-021 — Calibrated simulation with uncertainty

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-021`. Disposition: `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/20`.

**Mature form.** Calibrate the model to the relevant case mix, resource behaviour and current work state; validate predictions against observations and report uncertainty and sensitivity. Treat scenarios as conditional forecasts, not observed consequences of an intervention.

**Controlled failure.** Precisely wrong forecasts, warm-up mismatch, unrepresented work, narrow intervals with poor coverage and overfitting.

**Trigger.** Alternatives are costly or disruptive to test directly, or queues/current cases make short-term effects non-obvious.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use arithmetic, a case timeline or a limited real-world trial when these answer the decision adequately.

**Prerequisites.** A representationally adequate model, credible input data, an evaluation baseline and awareness of model/data drift.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Alternatives are costly or disruptive to test directly, or queues/current cases make short-term effects non-obvious.; consumer and authority: Analysts own model assumptions; operational decision makers decide whether the conditional evidence justifies action.

**Authority boundary.** Analysts own model assumptions; operational decision makers decide whether the conditional evidence justifies action.

**Required communication.** Communicate the baseline, predicted range, uncertain assumptions and alternatives to those making the intervention decision.

**Observable postcondition.** A simulation-supported choice includes an empirical calibration check and conditions under which the prediction is not relied upon.

**Criticism.** S018 finds a sharpness/coverage trade-off and assumes consistent complete event data; S027 could not validate all successful-process models. Simulation is not a causal substitute for the missing world.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The simulated case/resource system and the actual process to which a forecast is transferred.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Decision makers and the workers/recipients bearing forecast-error costs.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Check modelled state, timestamps and behaviour against real ongoing cases.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Calibrated simulation with explicit alternative scenarios and uncertainty, not an animation alone.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Prediction error, interval coverage and decision sensitivity; outcome effects need later observation.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Calibrate the model to the relevant case mix, resource behaviour and current work state; validate predictions against observations and report uncertainty and sensitivity. Treat scenarios as conditional forecasts, not observed consequences of an intervention.; causal limit: S018 finds a sharpness/coverage trade-off and assumes consistent complete event data; S027 could not validate all successful-process models. Simulation is not a causal substitute for the missing world.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Analysts own model assumptions; operational decision makers decide whether the conditional evidence justifies action.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Human allocation, adaptation and exception behaviour are model assumptions, not constants by definition.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated simulation or optimisation does not authorise deployment of its preferred alternative.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Calibration failure, current-state mismatch, changed resources or outcome drift.

### EBPM-022 — Causal restraint in intervention selection

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-022`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/21`.

**Mature form.** Distinguish description, prediction and causal intervention claims. State the proposed mechanism, plausible confounding and comparison; use stronger designs or bounded trials when an observational association could recommend the wrong intervention.

**Controlled failure.** Selection bias, conditioning on colliders, hidden common causes and reverse causation.

**Trigger.** A variant, actor or activity is blamed for an outcome, or a predicted association is used to choose a change.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A modest reversible trial with explicit observations can be more informative than a complex causal model based on weak assumptions.

**Prerequisites.** A defined intervention and outcome; relevant case mix; an honest account of what identifies causation and what remains assumed.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A variant, actor or activity is blamed for an outcome, or a predicted association is used to choose a change.; consumer and authority: Analysts can identify evidence limits; authorised owners decide on precaution, experimentation or non-intervention.

**Authority boundary.** Analysts can identify evidence limits; authorised owners decide on precaution, experimentation or non-intervention.

**Required communication.** Explain why the proposed comparison distinguishes the causal story from plausible rivals.

**Observable postcondition.** The decision states whether evidence is descriptive, predictive or causally identifying, with material alternative explanations visible.

**Criticism.** S019’s causal models have explicit assumptions; S031 and S040 are associational. The retained property is restraint, not a requirement to solve all causal identification before acting.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The proposed intervention and its downstream outcome, not just observed process variants.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** People receiving the intervention and those whose performance may be wrongly blamed.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Separate how work is recorded from why observed outcomes occur.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Mechanism sketch, rival explanations and an appropriate comparison design.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Intervention-relevant outcomes with uncertainty and case-mix treatment.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Distinguish description, prediction and causal intervention claims. State the proposed mechanism, plausible confounding and comparison; use stronger designs or bounded trials when an observational association could recommend the wrong intervention.; causal limit: S019’s causal models have explicit assumptions; S031 and S040 are associational. The retained property is restraint, not a requirement to solve all causal identification before acting.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Analysts can identify evidence limits; authorised owners decide on precaution, experimentation or non-intervention.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Exceptions may be responses to difficulty rather than causes of bad outcomes.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Predictive or causal-discovery software does not erase identification assumptions.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** An alternative explanation, changed case mix or outcome inconsistent with the proposed mechanism.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Separate how work is recorded from why observed outcomes occur. Measure: Intervention-relevant outcomes with uncertainty and case-mix treatment.

**Anti-ceremony and retirement boundary.** protected function: The decision states whether evidence is descriptive, predictive or causally identifying, with material alternative explanations visible.; consumer: Analysts can identify evidence limits; authorised owners decide on precaution, experimentation or non-intervention.; minimal adequate alternative: A modest reversible trial with explicit observations can be more informative than a complex causal model based on weak assumptions.; fuller form trigger: A variant, actor or activity is blamed for an outcome, or a predicted association is used to choose a change.; prerequisites: A defined intervention and outcome; relevant case mix; an honest account of what identifies causation and what remains assumed.; simplification risk: Selection bias, conditioning on colliders, hidden common causes and reverse causation.; omission or retirement condition: Omit when the stated trigger is absent, an existing/cheaper mechanism supplies the required function, or its risk/consumer/dependency has disappeared. Reconsider on: An alternative explanation, changed case mix or outcome inconsistent with the proposed mechanism.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-023 — Hypothesis-led comparison of redesign alternatives

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-023`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/22`.

**Mature form.** Form a specific change hypothesis and compare it with plausible organisational, informational and technical alternatives, including doing nothing. Define what would count as improvement, what may not worsen and how the choice will be evaluated after enactment.

**Controlled failure.** Confirmation bias, omitted cheap alternatives, unmeasurable success criteria and uncontrolled concurrent changes.

**Trigger.** A process intervention is proposed and multiple mechanisms could address the problem.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A small reversible change with a simple comparison may be enough; no full redesign workshop or simulation is necessary.

**Prerequisites.** A supported problem account, feasible alternatives, protected constraints and an evaluation route.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A process intervention is proposed and multiple mechanisms could address the problem.; consumer and authority: Owners/sponsors authorise changes within their remit; performers and recipients contribute feasibility and harm evidence.

**Authority boundary.** Owners/sponsors authorise changes within their remit; performers and recipients contribute feasibility and harm evidence.

**Required communication.** Communicate the rationale, alternatives rejected, expected effects and conditions for stopping or revising the change.

**Observable postcondition.** A selected redesign has an explicit mechanism, comparison and evaluable consequences rather than a technology-first justification.

**Criticism.** S027 shows deployment selection and concurrent conditions matter; S030 challenges narrow optimisation criteria. A plausible hypothesis is still not demonstrated benefit.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A bounded intervention and the real process outcomes it is intended to change.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Sponsors, performers, recipients and parties exposed to transition costs.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** The as-is baseline reflects performed work, including useful informal coordination.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Alternative descriptions and explicit hypotheses; use detailed models only where needed.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcome improvement subject to protected constraints and implementation cost.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Form a specific change hypothesis and compare it with plausible organisational, informational and technical alternatives, including doing nothing. Define what would count as improvement, what may not worsen and how the choice will be evaluated after enactment.; causal limit: S027 shows deployment selection and concurrent conditions matter; S030 challenges narrow optimisation criteria. A plausible hypothesis is still not demonstrated benefit.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Owners/sponsors authorise changes within their remit; performers and recipients contribute feasibility and harm evidence.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Compare standardisation with support for legitimate discretionary action.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Manual, organisational, information and automated options compete on the same outcome basis.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Unexpected harm, failure of the mechanism, new evidence or an alternative becoming cheaper.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** The as-is baseline reflects performed work, including useful informal coordination. Measure: Outcome improvement subject to protected constraints and implementation cost.

### EBPM-024 — Control-aware task elimination

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-024`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/23`.

**Mature form.** Before deleting a task, identify both its productive and protective functions, their consumers, and whether another mechanism preserves necessary assurance. Compare avoided effort with the error or accountability exposure created by removal.

**Controlled failure.** A rarely used safety function disappears; undocumented error correction is mistaken for waste.

**Trigger.** A step is labelled waste, redundant approval or avoidable checking.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Retain a cheap effective check when redesign cost or uncertain risk exceeds its burden; combine duplicate checks only after their distinct functions are understood.

**Prerequisites.** Evidence of the task’s actual use, controlled risks and replacement coverage.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A step is labelled waste, redundant approval or avoidable checking.; consumer and authority: The owner can propose deletion; the party accountable for the controlled consequence must be involved in accepting the changed exposure.

**Authority boundary.** The owner can propose deletion; the party accountable for the controlled consequence must be involved in accepting the changed exposure.

**Required communication.** Performers explain exceptions and hidden checking work to the redesign decision-maker.

**Observable postcondition.** The removed task’s necessary functions are either explicitly retired with reasons or demonstrably covered elsewhere.

**Criticism.** S016 reports practical constraints on redesign heuristics, including segregation of duties; universal elimination ignores those prerequisites.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The task and the downstream consequences it helps control.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Performers, recipients and people exposed to an undetected error.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Observe what the task really does, not only its stated label.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A function-and-consumer table may replace a detailed process diagram.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Avoided effort together with errors, recovery and harm exposure.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Before deleting a task, identify both its productive and protective functions, their consumers, and whether another mechanism preserves necessary assurance. Compare avoided effort with the error or accountability exposure created by removal.; causal limit: S016 reports practical constraints on redesign heuristics, including segregation of duties; universal elimination ignores those prerequisites.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** The owner can propose deletion; the party accountable for the controlled consequence must be involved in accepting the changed exposure.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Retain justified human judgement that a replacement cannot supply.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** An automated check is a substitute only for the functions it actually covers.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** New failure, previously hidden consumer or changed risk after removal.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Observe what the task really does, not only its stated label. Measure: Avoided effort together with errors, recovery and harm exposure.

Reconsider on: New failure, previously hidden consumer or changed risk after removal.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-025 — Dependency-aware resequencing

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-025`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/24`.

**Mature form.** Move an activity only after checking information, resource, commitment and protective dependencies. Early screening can avoid downstream effort, while premature action can create irrecoverable commitments.

**Controlled failure.** Early decisions use incomplete information; an apparent independence masks a legal or physical dependency.

**Trigger.** Cases commonly fail late or an ordering constraint appears unnecessary.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Keep the current order when dependencies are tight or savings are negligible; try a small reversible ordering change first.

**Prerequisites.** A dependency account and evidence about case rejection, information availability and reversibility.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Cases commonly fail late or an ordering constraint appears unnecessary.; consumer and authority: Operational owners coordinate changes across the functions whose inputs or commitments move.

**Authority boundary.** Operational owners coordinate changes across the functions whose inputs or commitments move.

**Required communication.** Participants agree when inputs are reliable enough and when a case becomes committed.

**Observable postcondition.** The revised order avoids targeted delay or unnecessary work without invalidating required inputs or controls.

**Criticism.** Heuristic plausibility does not establish net benefit in a new case mix; S016 is limited practitioner evidence.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The reordered activities and affected upstream/downstream commitments.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Performers, applicants or customers and dependent teams.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Inspect actual input arrival and exception ordering, not only the prescribed sequence.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A dependency table or small process fragment.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Avoided downstream effort, elapsed time, erroneous early rejection and rework.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Move an activity only after checking information, resource, commitment and protective dependencies. **PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Operational owners coordinate changes across the functions whose inputs or commitments move.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Permit review where early information is insufficient.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated early decisions require a defined external commitment boundary.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Changed input reliability, case mix or downstream rejection pattern.

### EBPM-026 — Synchronisation-aware parallelisation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-026`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/25`.

**Mature form.** Execute genuinely independent activities concurrently while specifying how their outputs are joined, what happens on disagreement or partial failure, and whether resources can sustain the overlap.

**Controlled failure.** Synchronisation waits replace serial waits; shared resources overload; one branch makes another’s work obsolete.

**Trigger.** Serial waiting dominates and dependencies permit overlapping work.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Keep serial execution when coordination or rework dominates; overlap only the independent portion.

**Prerequisites.** Dependency independence, sufficient resources and a workable completion/reconciliation rule.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Serial waiting dominates and dependencies permit overlapping work.; consumer and authority: The owner arranges cross-team commitments; each performer retains authority over its own result.

**Authority boundary.** The owner arranges cross-team commitments; each performer retains authority over its own result.

**Required communication.** Exchange readiness, completion, failure and conflicting-output information, not merely task-start notifications.

**Observable postcondition.** Elapsed time improves without unaccounted duplicate work, incompatible outputs or stranded partial completion.

**Criticism.** Redesign accounts do not guarantee the independence or capacity assumptions; deployment evidence is mixed.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Concurrent branches, their shared resources and the joined business outcome.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Parallel performers and recipients waiting for a complete result.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Observe actual overlap, join delays and abandoned work.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Dependency/interaction representation at branch level.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** End-to-end lead time, resource effort, conflicting outputs and recovery.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Execute genuinely independent activities concurrently while specifying how their outputs are joined, what happens on disagreement or partial failure, and whether resources can sustain the overlap.; causal limit: Redesign accounts do not guarantee the independence or capacity assumptions; deployment evidence is mixed.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** The owner arranges cross-team commitments; each performer retains authority over its own result.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Humans need authority to stop or reconcile incompatible branches.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** System concurrency does not establish business-level successful completion.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Join failures, overload, demand changes or rising speculative rework.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Observe actual overlap, join delays and abandoned work. Measure: End-to-end lead time, resource effort, conflicting outputs and recovery.

Reconsider on: Join failures, overload, demand changes or rising speculative rework.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-027 — Case coordination and task composition

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-027`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/26`.

**Mature form.** Assign sufficient continuity of knowledge and responsibility to a case while retaining necessary specialist review and segregation of duties. Compare fewer handoffs with the competence and concentration-of-authority costs of task combination.

**Controlled failure.** A single overloaded coordinator becomes a bottleneck; accountability is concentrated without competence.

**Trigger.** Cases repeatedly lose context or stall between fragmented responsibilities.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A shared case note and named coordinator can be enough; do not require one person to perform every specialist task.

**Prerequisites.** Competence, time, access to information and a legitimate division between coordination and decision powers.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Cases repeatedly lose context or stall between fragmented responsibilities.; consumer and authority: A coordinator may organise contributions without acquiring every professional or financial approval power.

**Authority boundary.** A coordinator may organise contributions without acquiring every professional or financial approval power.

**Required communication.** Specialists and coordinators communicate decisions, unresolved issues and recipient needs.

**Observable postcondition.** Case continuity improves while required specialist/control functions remain effective.

**Criticism.** S016 explicitly discusses training and segregation-of-duties constraints; S026 cautions against formalism that degrades professional judgement.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Case recipients, coordinators, specialists and control functions.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Inspect lost context and informal coordination currently repairing handoffs.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Case responsibility and unresolved-issue representation, not necessarily one serial flow.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Assign sufficient continuity of knowledge and responsibility to a case while retaining necessary specialist review and segregation of duties. **AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Shared software may support continuity but cannot confer professional authority.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Coordinator overload, skill gaps or recurrent specialist reversals.

### EBPM-028 — Conditional pooling and centralisation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-028`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/27`.

**Mature form.** Compare shared capacity and consistency benefits with queueing, local-knowledge loss, authority distance and a larger failure domain. Select central, local or federated arrangements according to the actual dependency and demand structure.

**Controlled failure.** The centre becomes a bottleneck; local work is displaced off-system; standard priorities exclude uncommon needs.

**Trigger.** Duplicated capabilities or uneven demand suggest a pooling opportunity.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Retain local provision when responsiveness, contextual expertise or failure isolation dominates.

**Prerequisites.** Demand/resource evidence, service commitments and agreement on allocation priorities.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Duplicated capabilities or uneven demand suggest a pooling opportunity.; consumer and authority: Shared-service governance must have authority to allocate capacity and a route for local concerns.

**Authority boundary.** Shared-service governance must have authority to allocate capacity and a route for local concerns.

**Required communication.** Local units expose demand and exceptional needs; the pooled service exposes capacity and priority decisions.

**Observable postcondition.** Pooling produces a defensible overall result, including access and delay for small or exceptional groups.

**Criticism.** S021 is a design framework, not proof of one superior configuration; S016 heuristics are conditional practitioner judgements.

### EBPM-029 — Case segmentation and skill allocation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-029`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/28`.

**Mature form.** Separate materially different cases where this improves fit, but validate classification quality and the burdens imposed on difficult or disadvantaged cases. Reassess class boundaries when demand changes.

**Controlled failure.** Easy cases improve while hard cases deteriorate; labels encode bias; specialist queues accumulate.

**Trigger.** A common route performs poorly for distinct needs, risks or required expertise.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Keep one route with an exception option when classification adds more error or delay than it removes.

**Prerequisites.** Observable distinguishing features, capacity for each class and a route for misclassified cases.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A common route performs poorly for distinct needs, risks or required expertise.; consumer and authority: Classification and resource-allocation decisions require accountable authority; analysts do not define acceptable exclusion alone.

**Authority boundary.** Classification and resource-allocation decisions require accountable authority; analysts do not define acceptable exclusion alone.

**Required communication.** Recipients and performers can challenge classifications and reveal unmet needs.

**Observable postcondition.** Case groups receive fit-for-purpose handling with visible misclassification and unequal-service effects.

**Criticism.** S030 supplies normative fairness constraints; redesign survey evidence does not establish distributional benefit.

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Case classes, their routes and the allocation boundary.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** All case groups, including rare, complex and initially misclassified cases.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare labelled classes with actual needs and outcomes.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Decision table plus route differences where necessary.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcomes, waiting and error by class, not only overall throughput.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Separate materially different cases where this improves fit, but validate classification quality and the burdens imposed on difficult or disadvantaged cases. Reassess class boundaries when demand changes.; causal limit: S030 supplies normative fairness constraints; redesign survey evidence does not establish distributional benefit.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Allow reassignment and expert override with reasons.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated triage remains a consequential decision requiring independent evaluation.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Misclassification, distributional harm or shifting case mix.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Compare labelled classes with actual needs and outcomes. Measure: Outcomes, waiting and error by class, not only overall throughput.

### EBPM-030 — Proportionate radical or incremental change

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-030`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/29`.

**Mature form.** Choose the scale and pace of change by the inadequacy of the current mechanism, uncertainty, transition risk and continuity needs. Consider radical replacement, incremental repair, mixed migration and no change as genuine alternatives.

**Controlled failure.** Radicalism becomes fashion; incrementalism preserves an unworkable structure; migration is underfunded.

**Trigger.** The current process is being reconsidered or incremental changes repeatedly fail to address a structural problem.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Retain or repair a functioning process when disruption has no warranted payoff.

**Prerequisites.** Baseline understanding, transition capacity and a way to evaluate the intended consequence.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. The current process is being reconsidered or incremental changes repeatedly fail to address a structural problem.; consumer and authority: Sponsors authorise strategic disruption; performers and affected recipients contribute transition and continuity evidence.

**Authority boundary.** Sponsors authorise strategic disruption; performers and affected recipients contribute transition and continuity evidence.

**Required communication.** Make reasons for change, protected services and unresolved risks understandable to affected actors.

**Observable postcondition.** The chosen change scale matches the problem and includes credible continuity and evaluation arrangements.

**Criticism.** S038 questions BPR novelty and unrealistic organisational claims; S027 records implementation losses rather than assuming deployment.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The process and its transition from current to proposed arrangements.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Sponsors, employees, recipients and partners exposed to disruption.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Baseline work and informal protections must be understood before replacement.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Alternative and transition accounts proportionate to uncertainty.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcome improvement, transition cost, continuity and recoverability.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Choose the scale and pace of change by the inadequacy of the current mechanism, uncertainty, transition risk and continuity needs. **PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Sponsors authorise strategic disruption; performers and affected recipients contribute transition and continuity evidence.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Retain human intervention during uncertain migration.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Technical deployment is only one component of organisational transition.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Failed milestones, service harm or evidence invalidating the redesign rationale.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Baseline work and informal protections must be understood before replacement. Measure: Outcome improvement, transition cost, continuity and recoverability.

### EBPM-031 — Process ownership with effective authority

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-031`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/30`.

**Mature form.** Connect responsibility for the process outcome with sufficient influence, knowledge, resources and an escalation route across participating functions. Separate an owner title from the powers actually exercised.

**Controlled failure.** Responsibility without resources; ceremonial titles; competing hierarchies; owners bypass legitimate specialist authority.

**Trigger.** An end-to-end problem crosses functional authority or an owner is assigned responsibility.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** In a small team, existing management and a clear escalation agreement may suffice; no extra role is required.

**Prerequisites.** Recognised decision rights, information access, resource commitments and a legitimate resolution path for conflicts.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. An end-to-end problem crosses functional authority or an owner is assigned responsibility.; consumer and authority: The process owner acts within delegated powers; functional and professional authorities retain powers not transferred. Conflicts require negotiation or escalation, not fictitious unilateral control.

**Authority boundary.** The process owner acts within delegated powers; functional and professional authorities retain powers not transferred. Conflicts require negotiation or escalation, not fictitious unilateral control.

**Required communication.** Owners, functional managers, performers and sponsors exchange priorities, constraints, evidence and unresolved decisions.

**Observable postcondition.** A consequential cross-functional decision can actually be made and implemented by identified actors.

**Criticism.** S020’s field account exposes the gap between nominal and exercised ownership; S027 shows management and organisational conditions affect implementation.

### EBPM-032 — Strategy-to-process translation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-032`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/31`.

**Mature form.** Translate strategic commitments into explicit process outcomes, selection priorities and constraints. Examine conflicts between the sponsor’s strategy, affected-party interests and actual operational capacity rather than assuming alignment is inherently beneficial.

**Controlled failure.** Vague alignment language; strategy serves only powerful stakeholders; cascading targets distort work.

**Trigger.** Processes compete for investment or strategic objectives change.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A small service can use a few agreed outcome priorities without a formal strategy cascade.

**Prerequisites.** An intelligible strategy, credible operational evidence and a legitimate way to decide competing priorities.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Processes compete for investment or strategic objectives change.; consumer and authority: Strategy sponsors decide commitments; owners and performers test feasibility; affected parties inform consequences.

**Authority boundary.** Strategy sponsors decide commitments; owners and performers test feasibility; affected parties inform consequences.

**Observable postcondition.** Investment and redesign choices have a traceable substantive reason and can be challenged when consequences contradict it.

**Criticism.** S030 challenges efficiency-only responsibility; S040 observes association, not proof that maturity or strategic alignment causes sustainability.

### EBPM-033 — Context-fit BPM governance configuration

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-033`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/32`.

**Mature form.** Select centralised, decentralised or federated governance according to process interdependence, organisational context and available capabilities. Specify the decisions each arrangement enables rather than treating a BPM office as a universal requirement.

**Controlled failure.** Central bureaucracy; disconnected local practice; unclear federated responsibility; governance consumes more than it protects.

**Trigger.** Multiple units need coordination, common methods or shared process investment.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use existing management and a few explicit coordination agreements when they resolve the actual decisions.

**Prerequisites.** Knowledge of organisational dependencies, available competencies and the authority needed for the selected scope.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Multiple units need coordination, common methods or shared process investment.; consumer and authority: A BPM office may advise, enable or govern only within its mandate; process and functional managers retain explicitly allocated decisions.

**Authority boundary.** A BPM office may advise, enable or govern only within its mandate; process and functional managers retain explicitly allocated decisions.

**Required communication.** Exchange local evidence with shared standards or investment decisions, including a route to contest misfit.

**Observable postcondition.** The chosen arrangement resolves identified coordination needs without unexplained duplication or authority gaps.

**Criticism.** S021 offers a context-oriented design account, not comparative proof of a best form; S023 challenges rigid maturity paths.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The organisation’s process-management decisions, not every operational act.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** BPM specialists, owners, functional teams and process recipients.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Inspect actual support and decisions rather than existence of a centre or policy.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Governance configuration and interaction map at the decision level.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Decision effectiveness, support quality, coordination cost and process consequences.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Select centralised, decentralised or federated governance according to process interdependence, organisational context and available capabilities. Specify the decisions each arrangement enables rather than treating a BPM office as a universal requirement.; causal limit: S021 offers a context-oriented design account, not comparative proof of a best form; S023 challenges rigid maturity paths.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** A BPM office may advise, enable or govern only within its mandate; process and functional managers retain explicitly allocated decisions.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Local expertise must have an effective exception or challenge route.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Tool administration and process governance are separate authority surfaces.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Unresolved ownership, duplicated work, local bypass or changed organisational dependencies.

### EBPM-034 — Capability assessment as fallible diagnosis

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-034`. Disposition: `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/33`.

**Mature form.** Use a capability assessment as a fallible diagnostic hypothesis about specific competencies, not as a proxy for process success or an obligatory stage ladder. Check evidence behind ratings and whether the proposed development addresses a real outcome problem.

**Controlled failure.** Gaming; self-report bias; arbitrary stages; consultant dependence; capability scores rise while outcomes deteriorate.

**Trigger.** An organisation needs to diagnose capability gaps or choose improvement support.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Investigate the actual failed decisions or processes directly when a broad assessment would not change action.

**Prerequisites.** A fit-for-purpose model, observable rating evidence, multiple relevant perspectives and an outcome-based consumer.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. An organisation needs to diagnose capability gaps or choose improvement support.; consumer and authority: Assessors describe evidence and limits; sponsors decide whether an identified capability warrants investment.

**Authority boundary.** Assessors describe evidence and limits; sponsors decide whether an identified capability warrants investment.

**Required communication.** Participants can challenge ratings and distinguish missing paperwork from missing capability.

**Observable postcondition.** Assessment produces testable development priorities or an explicit decision that no further machinery is warranted.

**Criticism.** S022 and S023 identify weak validation and prescriptive limits. S040 adds positive cross-sectional ESG-readiness associations but does not establish causal or environmental benefit.

### EBPM-035 — Participation, learning and incentive alignment

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-035`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/34`.

**Mature form.** Involve those performing and receiving work in discovery, redesign and evaluation; provide skills and incentives that permit the intended behaviour. Treat participation as consequential influence and learning, not attendance or consultation without effect.

**Controlled failure.** Token involvement; coercive consensus; training without time or tools; cultural ratings substitute for changed conditions.

**Trigger.** A change depends on new behaviour, tacit knowledge or acceptance by affected actors.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Direct discussion and guided practice may suffice for a small change; a formal change programme is not always needed.

**Prerequisites.** Time to participate, safe expression of disagreement and a route by which contributions affect decisions.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A change depends on new behaviour, tacit knowledge or acceptance by affected actors.; consumer and authority: Sponsors resource participation; performers contribute operational knowledge; consultation does not erase formal accountability.

**Authority boundary.** Sponsors resource participation; performers contribute operational knowledge; consultation does not erase formal accountability.

**Required communication.** Explain proposed changes and respond visibly to objections, uncertainty and practical constraints.

**Observable postcondition.** Relevant knowledge changes the design or is explicitly adjudicated, and actors can perform the new work.

**Criticism.** S024’s municipal survey supports an association-based account, not a causal guarantee from culture interventions; S026 and S041 expose work hidden by formal designs.

### EBPM-036 — Bounded human discretion and exceptions

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-036`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/35`.

**Mature form.** Distinguish legitimate discretion from uncontrolled deviation. Define the goals and constraints that remain binding, who can depart from the ordinary route, and how the decision and its consequences are communicated and reviewed.

**Controlled failure.** Rigid rules cause harm; unrestricted discretion causes inconsistency, bias or untraceable decisions.

**Trigger.** Case facts invalidate the normal route or expertise is needed beyond a fixed activity sequence.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For low-risk, simple work, a clear goal and accessible expert may suffice without a case-management platform.

**Prerequisites.** Competent actors, intelligible constraints and a way to communicate and review consequential departures.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Case facts invalidate the normal route or expertise is needed beyond a fixed activity sequence.; consumer and authority: A case worker’s discretion is bounded by professional and organisational authority; a model’s optional task does not confer legal or ethical permission.

**Authority boundary.** A case worker’s discretion is bounded by professional and organisational authority; a model’s optional task does not confer legal or ethical permission.

**Required communication.** Communicate exceptional facts, chosen departure and unresolved obligations to those affected or responsible.

**Observable postcondition.** The case can proceed or stop for defensible reasons without losing necessary accountability.

**Criticism.** S026 criticises bureaucratic formalism; S030 raises fairness and accountability. Neither proves every workaround is legitimate.

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** A case whose circumstances exceed the ordinary procedure.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Goals, constraints, case information and decision reasons; a fixed sequence may be inappropriate.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Discretion is the central mechanism; competence and authority cannot be inferred from software access.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Human override must correspond to real operational control, not a cosmetic button.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Recurring exceptions, harmful variation or changed professional knowledge.

### EBPM-037 — Workaround-driven misalignment inquiry

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-037`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/36`.

**Mature form.** Investigate a workaround’s cause, protected purpose, risks and affected parties before either suppressing it or institutionalising it. Distinguish useful repair, constraint evasion and temporary accommodation, then decide whether to change the system, the work or neither.

**Controlled failure.** Successful local repair hides system defects; normalisation of unsafe evasion; punishment suppresses reporting.

**Trigger.** Actors repeatedly bypass a prescribed procedure or information system.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Resolve an isolated, understood mismatch locally when no wider change is justified.

**Prerequisites.** Access to practitioners’ accounts and consequences, protection against premature blame, and knowledge of the bypassed control.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Actors repeatedly bypass a prescribed procedure or information system.; consumer and authority: Performers can explain the workaround; authorised owners and relevant control holders decide its acceptance or replacement.

**Authority boundary.** Performers can explain the workaround; authorised owners and relevant control holders decide its acceptance or replacement.

**Required communication.** Connect the reason for the bypass with the people responsible for the underlying misfit and those bearing the risk.

**Observable postcondition.** The workaround receives an evidence-based disposition and material unresolved consequences are visible.

**Criticism.** S025 is case-based and shares ancestry with earlier work, not independent population replication; S041 shows workarounds can coexist with necessary formal accountability.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The prescribed process, actual bypass and the function each serves.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Practitioners, recipients, system designers and control/accountability consumers.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** The discrepancy itself is the evidence object; neither description automatically wins.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Case narrative and discrepancy map may be sufficient.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Repeated recurrence, harm, change in the bypassed risk or discovery of a cheaper legitimate route.

### EBPM-038 — Responsible visibility and affected-person protection

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-038`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/37`.

**Mature form.** Limit process visibility and automated judgements to a declared purpose with appropriate access, participation and challenge. Examine privacy, surveillance, fairness and worker/recipient burdens as design consequences, not external afterthoughts.

**Controlled failure.** Function creep, coerced consent, biased proxies, chilling of useful informal coordination and asymmetric visibility.

**Trigger.** Monitoring, employee-level data, sensitive case information or consequential algorithmic decisions are introduced.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use aggregate, sampled or less identifying evidence when it answers the decision adequately; omit monitoring with no legitimate consumer.

**Prerequisites.** A justified information purpose, proportionate access and a meaningful route for affected actors to contest error or misuse.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Monitoring, employee-level data, sensitive case information or consequential algorithmic decisions are introduced.; consumer and authority: Managers do not acquire unlimited surveillance authority through technical availability; applicable professional, organisational and legal constraints require separate determination.

**Authority boundary.** Managers do not acquire unlimited surveillance authority through technical availability; applicable professional, organisational and legal constraints require separate determination.

**Required communication.** Affected people understand consequential uses of data and can raise errors and adverse effects.

**Observable postcondition.** The evidence used for decisions has a defensible purpose and access boundary, with harms and disputes visible.

**Criticism.** S030 is a normative/conceptual contribution, not an effectiveness trial; S026 and S041 show how formal visibility can distort work.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The process and the people represented in its data and decisions.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Workers, customers, vulnerable recipients, data users and indirectly affected people.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Logged traces omit context and must not be treated as a complete individual performance account.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Information-use and decision-impact account, with appropriate aggregation.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcome quality, distributional effects, privacy burden and contestability.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Limit process visibility and automated judgements to a declared purpose with appropriate access, participation and challenge. Examine privacy, surveillance, fairness and worker/recipient burdens as design consequences, not external afterthoughts.; causal limit: S030 is a normative/conceptual contribution, not an effectiveness trial; S026 and S041 show how formal visibility can distort work.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Preserve human challenge and avoid penalising justified exceptions mechanically.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Learned or automated decisions require independent validation and accountable intervention.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** New data use, harmful inference, access expansion or affected-party challenge.

### EBPM-039 — Incentive-aware appraisal and metric interpretation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-039`. Disposition: `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED`. Examination: `EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/38`.

**Mature form.** Interpret indicators in light of the behaviour they reward, omitted work and differences in case difficulty. Use multiple relevant observations and inquiry into gaming rather than assuming the measured quantity remains a neutral description once tied to appraisal.

**Controlled failure.** Gaming, cherry-picking easy cases, premature closure, hidden rework and suppression of bad news.

**Trigger.** A measure affects pay, ranking, sanctions, targets or resource access.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Use qualitative review or aggregate learning measures where precise individual attribution is not warranted.

**Prerequisites.** Knowledge of incentives, case mix and how work can move outside the measurement boundary.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A measure affects pay, ranking, sanctions, targets or resource access.; consumer and authority: Appraisal decision-makers are accountable for the use of measures; analysts must expose limitations rather than authorising sanctions.

**Authority boundary.** Appraisal decision-makers are accountable for the use of measures; analysts must expose limitations rather than authorising sanctions.

**Required communication.** Workers and recipients can explain discrepant indicators and hidden burden without the discrepancy alone proving misconduct.

**Observable postcondition.** An appraisal or improvement decision accounts for known distortions and does not equate target attainment with the entire outcome.

**Criticism.** The direct causal magnitude of incentive effects is not estimated by the inspected BPM evidence; this is a source-grounded analytical safeguard, not a validated universal scoring scheme.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The measured process plus work displaced by target pursuit.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Measured employees, recipients, managers and excluded or difficult cases.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare logged attainment with case narratives and received outcomes.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** An indicator-purpose-use table can be sufficient.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcome and distribution, including abandonment, rework and unmeasured effort.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Appraisal decision-makers are accountable for the use of measures; analysts must expose limitations rather than authorising sanctions.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Do not treat every justified exception as adverse performance.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Automated scoring can scale a flawed attribution mechanism.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Target attainment without outcome gain, unusual case selection or reporting anomalies.

### EBPM-040 — Socio-technical enactment and adoption

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-040`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/39`.

**Mature form.** Treat enactment as the joint establishment of working technology, roles, skills, resources, coordination and actual adoption. Verify that actors can perform the intended work and that the organisation receives the outcome; deployment is only one observation.

**Controlled failure.** Technically live but unused system; abandoned implementation; shadow work; disruption of necessary informal coordination.

**Trigger.** A to-be design is introduced or a new system is claimed to improve a process.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** An organisational agreement, training or information change may suffice without new software.

**Prerequisites.** Operational readiness, integration with surrounding work, accountable authority and an evaluation baseline.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A to-be design is introduced or a new system is claimed to improve a process.; consumer and authority: Sponsors resource change; owners coordinate adoption; technical teams control only technical deployment within their mandate.

**Authority boundary.** Sponsors resource change; owners coordinate adoption; technical teams control only technical deployment within their mandate.

**Required communication.** Performers, support staff, partners and recipients exchange readiness, problems and unresolved work during transition.

**Observable postcondition.** Real cases are completed under the new arrangement with responsibilities and observed consequences understood.

**Criticism.** S027 followed 25 intended processes but only seven implemented processes were evaluated; selection and concurrent change limit benefit claims.

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The redesigned process and the surrounding organisation that must make it work.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Performers, technical/support teams, recipients and dependent organisations.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Observe real use and off-system work instead of equating installation with adoption.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Readiness and actual-case evidence, with models suited to the change.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Executable workflow is a component, not the organisational process itself.

### EBPM-041 — Selective automation and interface suitability

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-041`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/40`.

**Mature form.** Select automation according to process stability, interface characteristics, data quality, exception load and the value of human judgement. Compare interface-level RPA, deeper integration and non-automation; test the actual changed work rather than relying on a tool category.

**Controlled failure.** Fragile screen automation, accelerated flawed decisions, hidden exceptions and maintenance cost omitted from benefit claims.

**Trigger.** Repetitive digital work or coordination overhead suggests a technical intervention.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Retain manual work or improve information/organisation when exception cost or integration fragility outweighs automation benefit.

**Prerequisites.** Adequate inputs, known interfaces, operation/support capacity and a meaningful outcome test.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Repetitive digital work or coordination overhead suggests a technical intervention.; consumer and authority: Automation executes delegated operations; it does not establish the legitimacy of the underlying business decision.

**Authority boundary.** Automation executes delegated operations; it does not establish the legitimacy of the underlying business decision.

**Required communication.** Users, technical operators and owners communicate interface changes, exceptions and consequences.

**Observable postcondition.** The selected mechanism performs its bounded function under stated conditions, with business consequences evaluated separately.

### EBPM-042 — External-effect and completion reconciliation

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-042`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/41`.

**Mature form.** Distinguish a local task or engine completion from completion of the obligation to a recipient or partner. Identify the external effects that matter and obtain appropriate receiving-side or operational evidence before declaring the business case complete.

**Controlled failure.** Local success masks failed delivery, duplicate effect, unresolved liability or manual repair.

**Trigger.** A process sends messages, transfers goods or money, changes external records or closes an obligation.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A direct recipient confirmation or simple reconciliation may suffice; do not require a new distributed protocol for every process.

**Prerequisites.** An agreed business completion meaning, identifiable external dependencies and access to proportionate confirmation evidence.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A process sends messages, transfers goods or money, changes external records or closes an obligation.; consumer and authority: Each actor controls its own commitments; a local owner cannot declare another party’s obligations fulfilled by fiat.

**Authority boundary.** Each actor controls its own commitments; a local owner cannot declare another party’s obligations fulfilled by fiat.

**Required communication.** Communicate acceptance, rejection, missing effects and outstanding responsibility across interfaces.

**Observable postcondition.** Declared completion corresponds to the agreed business outcome, or remaining uncertainty and responsibility are explicit.

**Criticism.** BPMN execution semantics cover a defined technical scope and may ignore non-operational elements; they do not prove organisational completion. S028 validates governance modelling, not all network outcomes.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The local case plus materially relevant external effects and obligations.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Originating and receiving actors, including those bearing failed or duplicate effects.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Compare system completion with actual receipt and remaining manual work.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Completion/obligation and interface account.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Received result, unresolved obligations and recovery burden.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Distinguish a local task or engine completion from completion of the obligation to a recipient or partner. Identify the external effects that matter and obtain appropriate receiving-side or operational evidence before declaring the business case complete.; causal limit: BPMN execution semantics cover a defined technical scope and may ignore non-operational elements; they do not prove organisational completion. S028 validates governance modelling, not all network outcomes.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Each actor controls its own commitments; a local owner cannot declare another party’s obligations fulfilled by fiat.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Humans may need to reconcile ambiguous completion rather than automatically retry or close.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** External consequences remain consequential even outside the engine’s observable state.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Unconfirmed receipt, disputed completion, duplicate effect or outstanding obligation.

**Evidence a later target must supply.** Compare system completion with actual receipt and remaining manual work. Measure: Received result, unresolved obligations and recovery burden.

**Anti-ceremony and retirement boundary.** protected function: Declared completion corresponds to the agreed business outcome, or remaining uncertainty and responsibility are explicit.; consumer: Each actor controls its own commitments; a local owner cannot declare another party’s obligations fulfilled by fiat.; minimal adequate alternative: A direct recipient confirmation or simple reconciliation may suffice; do not require a new distributed protocol for every process.; fuller form trigger: A process sends messages, transfers goods or money, changes external records or closes an obligation.; prerequisites: An agreed business completion meaning, identifiable external dependencies and access to proportionate confirmation evidence.; simplification risk: Local success masks failed delivery, duplicate effect, unresolved liability or manual repair.; omission or retirement condition: Omit when the stated trigger is absent, an existing/cheaper mechanism supplies the required function, or its risk/consumer/dependency has disappeared. Reconsider on: Unconfirmed receipt, disputed completion, duplicate effect or outstanding obligation.; anti ceremony boundary: Presence of an artefact, role, meeting, score or tool does not establish the observable postcondition; removing it must not destroy a real protective or accountability function.

### EBPM-043 — Recovery and controlled interruption

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-043`. Disposition: `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/42`.

**Mature form.** Where failed or interrupted work can leave consequential partial effects, ensure actors know how to pause, assess outstanding obligations and select an authorised continuation or recovery. The BPM-level requirement does not prescribe engine-specific retry, rollback or compensation algorithms.

**Controlled failure.** Blind retry duplicates effects; a stop leaves recipients stranded; nominal rollback cannot undo human or external consequences.

**Trigger.** Work can fail after consequential action or unsafe continuation is plausible.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** For a reversible low-impact task, manual stop-and-check with a responsible person may be sufficient.

**Prerequisites.** Visibility of partial work, reachable responsible actors and feasible recovery options.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Work can fail after consequential action or unsafe continuation is plausible.; consumer and authority: Operators may stop within delegated authority; reversal or compensation of another actor’s commitment requires the relevant authority.

**Authority boundary.** Operators may stop within delegated authority; reversal or compensation of another actor’s commitment requires the relevant authority.

**Required communication.** Communicate what happened, what remains uncertain and who owns unfinished obligations.

**Observable postcondition.** An interrupted case is not silently treated as completed, and an accountable decision governs continuation or resolution.

**Criticism.** The inspected specifications establish modelling/execution constructs, not universal organisational recovery success; S027 demonstrates limits of deployment evidence.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** Partial cases, external effects and their outstanding responsibilities.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Operators, recipients, partners and authorised recovery decision-makers.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Record actual partial effects, including those missing from the normal log.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** A recovery/continuity account proportional to consequence and reversibility.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Unresolved cases, harm prevented or caused by stopping, and recovery outcome.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Where failed or interrupted work can leave consequential partial effects, ensure actors know how to pause, assess outstanding obligations and select an authorised continuation or recovery. **HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Provide a practical route for manual judgement on ambiguous failures.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Technical cancellation does not imply reversal of an external business effect.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Failed observation, partial completion, unsafe continuation or exhausted recovery options.

### EBPM-044 — Decision-linked monitoring

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-044`. Disposition: `STRONGLY_RETAINED`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/43`.

**Mature form.** Connect each consequential indicator to a decision, a responsible consumer and a feasible action or inquiry. A dashboard can inform action but its production is not itself adaptation or improvement.

**Controlled failure.** Alert overload, ritual dashboards, absent authority and incentives to suppress exceptions.

**Trigger.** Process measures or monitoring are introduced or reviewed.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** Periodic case review or a simple threshold with a known responder may suffice; retire indicators with no current decision use.

**Prerequisites.** A valid measure, an actor able to respond and a defined reason to reconsider work.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Process measures or monitoring are introduced or reviewed.; consumer and authority: Monitoring analysts provide evidence; authorised owners or professionals decide interventions.

**Authority boundary.** Monitoring analysts provide evidence; authorised owners or professionals decide interventions.

**Required communication.** Transmit deviations, uncertainty and context to a consumer who can question the signal and act.

**Observable postcondition.** A relevant observation results in a documented decision, inquiry or reasoned non-action rather than passive reporting.

**Criticism.** S036 explicitly differentiates technical from organisational/ecosystem action; S041 shows formal accounts serve particular consumers rather than exhaustive truth.

### EBPM-045 — Drift-sensitive reassessment

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-045`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/44`.

**Mature form.** Reassess models, assumptions and measures when demand, case mix, resources, interfaces or actual practices change. Distinguish benign variation from a changed mechanism and avoid updating the model merely to normalise a harmful deviation.

**Controlled failure.** Drift detector chases noise; a new normal embeds harm; stale resource assumptions invalidate simulation.

**Trigger.** Observed patterns differ materially from the baseline or the operating environment changes.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A focused review of changed cases may suffice; continuous automated drift detection is unnecessary for infrequent stable work.

**Prerequisites.** A stated baseline and scope, relevant observations and knowledge of environmental changes.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. Observed patterns differ materially from the baseline or the operating environment changes.; consumer and authority: Analysts identify changed evidence; owners and affected actors decide whether models, operations or objectives need revision.

**Authority boundary.** Analysts identify changed evidence; owners and affected actors decide whether models, operations or objectives need revision.

**Required communication.** Communicate changed assumptions, affected cases and uncertainty rather than only a drift score.

**Observable postcondition.** The change is classified and consequential assumptions or controls are reviewed with reasons.

**Criticism.** S018 demonstrates technically richer simulation but does not remove future-demand uncertainty; S025’s workarounds are contextual rather than universal drift diagnoses.

#### Domain profile

**PROCESS_PURPOSE_AND_BOUNDARY.** The process and the environmental assumptions supporting its current design.

**BENEFICIARIES_AND_AFFECTED_ACTORS.** Owners, performers, recipients and partners whose conditions changed.

**WORK_AS_DESCRIBED_VERSUS_WORK_AS_PERFORMED.** Investigate changed practice and unlogged adaptations as well as event distributions.

**MODEL_PURPOSE_AND_ABSTRACTION.** Versioned assumptions and comparative case evidence; no mandatory full model refresh.

**PERFORMANCE_OR_OUTCOME_MEASURE.** Outcome shifts, case mix, resource availability and distributional consequences.

**REDESIGN_HYPOTHESIS_AND_CAUSAL_LIMIT.** hypothesis: Reassess models, assumptions and measures when demand, case mix, resources, interfaces or actual practices change. Distinguish benign variation from a changed mechanism and avoid updating the model merely to normalise a harmful deviation.; causal limit: S018 demonstrates technically richer simulation but does not remove future-demand uncertainty; S025’s workarounds are contextual rather than universal drift diagnoses.

**PROCESS_OWNER_AND_EFFECTIVE_AUTHORITY.** Analysts identify changed evidence; owners and affected actors decide whether models, operations or objectives need revision.

**HUMAN_DISCRETION_AND_EXCEPTION_HANDLING.** Recurring exceptions may indicate a changed process rather than individual error.

**AUTOMATION_AND_EXTERNAL_EFFECT_BOUNDARY.** Changing inputs can invalidate both learned and deterministic automation assumptions.

**FEEDBACK_AND_REVISION_TRIGGER.** Demand/resource change, new workaround pattern, interface revision or outcome drift.

### EBPM-046 — Outcome evaluation and control retirement

Global key: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT::EBPM-046`. Disposition: `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`. Examination: `EXAMINED`. Full record: `EVOLVED_BUSINESS_PROCESS_MANAGEMENT_PROPERTY_LEDGER.json#/properties/45`.

**Mature form.** Separate model change, implementation, adoption and measured outcome change. Review the proposed mechanism against observed consequences and retire, simplify or replace a control when its risk, consumer or payoff no longer warrants its cost.

**Controlled failure.** Sunk-cost retention; selective reporting; improvement credited to a model never used; controls accumulate after their purpose disappears.

**Trigger.** A change has been enacted, an evaluation point arrives, or a control’s continued value is questioned.

**Non-trigger and cheap path.** A small before/after case review may support a reversible local decision, with causal limits stated; no new intervention is an acceptable result.

**Prerequisites.** Baseline or credible comparison, evidence of actual use, relevant outcomes and knowledge of concurrent changes.

**Consumer and decision.** decision: Whether and how to apply the mechanism under its trigger, or reject its universalised use. A change has been enacted, an evaluation point arrives, or a control’s continued value is questioned.; consumer and authority: Owners and sponsors decide continuation within their powers; affected actors provide evidence of benefits and burdens.

**Authority boundary.** Owners and sponsors decide continuation within their powers; affected actors provide evidence of benefits and burdens.

**Required communication.** Report unfavourable and null outcomes alongside benefits, including who bears transition and ongoing costs.

**Observable postcondition.** The change receives a reasoned retain, revise, withdraw or evidence-insufficient decision.
